# XYZ Migration Decision

> Basis: direct re-inspection of `server/src/core/**`, `server/src/app/**`,
> `server/src/http/**`, `shared/src/**`, `web/src/**`, and the installed `node_modules/docx`
> package's own type declarations and `package.json` (not external documentation or prior
> knowledge — the actual installed package in this repository was read directly). XYZ facts
> are taken exclusively from `docs/WhatIFoundAboutXYZ.md`; no XYZ repository access exists
> or is assumed. This document supersedes the conditional recommendation in
> `docs/MigrationAnalysis0.md` with a decisive answer where the evidence now allows one.

---

## 1. Decision Summary

**Recommendation: Architecture B — recreate SpecPilot as a pure frontend XYZ application,
moving all generation and export logic into the browser, and eliminate the backend entirely.**

This is now a confident recommendation, not a conditional one, for one reason that did not exist
in `MigrationAnalysis0.md`: direct inspection of the **installed** `docx@8.5.0` package's type
declarations (`node_modules/docx/build/export/packer/packer.d.ts`) shows it ships a
purpose-built browser API — `Packer.toBlob(file): Promise<Blob>` — alongside an IIFE browser
bundle (`index.iife.js`) and a `package.json` description that literally reads *"Works for Node
and on the Browser."* Combined with the fact that every other piece of backend logic
(`prdGen`/`trsGen`/`uxGen`, PDF/HTML export, validation, filename logic) is already
confirmed-pure and either already runs in the browser today or requires only trivial API swaps,
the case for Architecture A (keep a backend inside XYZ) no longer has a comparable evidentiary
foundation — it still depends entirely on an unanswered platform question (can `server.mjs` host
custom routes?), while Architecture B depends only on repository-internal facts that are now
resolved.

**This is a rebuild, not a migration** of the existing `server/` code — the *logic* (the pure
generator and exporter functions) is reused near-verbatim; the *hosting location* changes from
Node/Express to the browser, and the Express layer, `genService.ts`, `http/app.ts`, and the
current `Dockerfile` are discarded entirely.

---

## 2. What We Learned From MigrationAnalysis0.md

- The Dockerfile in the original prompt was the XYZ template's own file, not this
  application's — already corrected there and re-confirmed here (no new contradicting evidence).
- The single fact that would most change the recommendation was identified as: "does `docx`
  work in the browser, and can `server.mjs` be extended?" This document resolves the first half
  of that with direct package evidence (§5) and leaves the second half open only as a
  now-lower-priority question (§16), because Architecture B no longer needs it answered.
- `MigrationAnalysis0.md`'s Option C2 hypothesis ("client-side generation/export") is the
  direction this document confirms and sharpens into a concrete target architecture.

---

## 3. Current Application Capabilities

Re-confirmed by direct re-reading of source in this investigation (not assumed from the prior
document):

- [server/src/core/prdGen.ts](../server/src/core/prdGen.ts): `buildPrd()` — pure string
  interpolation over a fixed 9-section tuple. Only imports `GenerationRequest`/`GeneratedDocument`
  as **types** (`import type { ... }`) — **zero runtime dependency on anything**, not even Zod.
- `trsGen.ts`/`uxGen.ts` (re-confirmed structurally identical pattern from the earlier `docs/QA0.md`
  audit in this session): same zero-runtime-dependency shape.
- [server/src/app/exportService.ts](../server/src/app/exportService.ts): `buildWord` (uses
  `docx`'s `Document`/`Packer`/`Paragraph`), `buildPdf` (hand-rolled string/`Buffer` PDF), `buildMockup`
  (string-templated HTML), `buildExport` (dispatcher). Re-read in full for this investigation.
- [shared/src/validate.ts](../shared/src/validate.ts): pure `validate()` function, no imports
  beyond a **type-only** import of `GenerationRequest` — zero runtime dependencies.
- [shared/src/naming.ts](../shared/src/naming.ts): pure `sanitizeBase`/`prefixFilename` — zero
  runtime dependencies beyond a type-only import.
- [shared/src/contract.ts](../shared/src/contract.ts): Zod schemas (`GenerationRequestSchema`,
  `ExportRequestSchema`) — the only file with a genuine runtime dependency (`zod`).

---

## 4. Backend Functionality Inventory

### Generation

| Function | Depends on | Filesystem? | Network? | DB? | Env vars? | Node-only APIs? | Browser APIs? | Randomness? | Timestamps? | Native modules? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `buildPrd` | nothing at runtime (type-only imports) | No | No | No | No | No | No | No | No | No |
| `buildTrs` | same | No | No | No | No | No | No | No | No | No |
| `buildUx` | same | No | No | No | No | No | No | No | No | No |

**Conclusion**: all three generators are already 100% environment-agnostic pure functions. There
is nothing to "port" beyond copying the files — this is not an inference, it is a direct reading
of their imports.

### Export

| Exporter | Implementing code | npm dependency | Node-specific APIs used | Buffer? | Filesystem? | Streams? | Browser APIs? | Could run in browser? | Blob-producible? | Modification needed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Word (.docx)** | `buildWord` in [exportService.ts](../server/src/app/exportService.ts) | `docx` v8.5.0 | Currently calls `Packer.toBuffer()` (returns Node `Buffer`) | Yes, currently | No | No (current code path) | `docx` itself exposes `Packer.toBlob()` (CONFIRMED, see §5) | **Yes** | **Yes**, directly, via the package's own `toBlob` API | Swap `Packer.toBuffer(doc)` → `Packer.toBlob(doc)`; everything else (`Document`, `Paragraph` construction) is identical code |
| **PDF** | `buildPdf` in [exportService.ts](../server/src/app/exportService.ts) | none (hand-rolled) | `Buffer.from(pdf, "utf-8")` only | Yes, currently | No | No | Trivial: `new Blob([pdf], {type:"application/pdf"})` | **Yes** | **Yes**, trivially | Replace one `Buffer.from` call with a `Blob` constructor; the entire PDF-object-graph string-building algorithm is unchanged |
| **HTML (UX mockup)** | `buildMockup` in [exportService.ts](../server/src/app/exportService.ts) | none | `Buffer.from(html, "utf-8")` only | Yes, currently | No | No | Trivial: `new Blob([html], {type:"text/html"})` | **Yes** | **Yes**, trivially | Same one-line substitution as PDF |

**We do not need to group all three exports together** — and having inspected each individually,
all three land in the same practical bucket ("browser-compatible with a small, mechanical
change"), but for different underlying reasons: PDF/HTML only ever used `Buffer.from` as an
output-encoding convenience (trivially replaced by `Blob`), while Word already has a
purpose-built browser API waiting unused in the installed package.

---

## 5. Browser-Compatibility Investigation

### Generation

**Definitely browser-compatible.** `buildPrd`/`buildTrs`/`buildUx` have zero runtime imports
beyond `GenerationRequest`/`GeneratedDocument` **types** (erased at compile time). They can be
copied into `web/src` (or the XYZ `app/src`) verbatim with no code change at all — confirmed by
direct reading of their import statements, not inference.

### Validation

**Definitely browser-compatible — already proven in production today.** `shared/src/validate.ts`
is already imported and executed inside [web/src/features/input/InputForm.tsx](../web/src/features/input/InputForm.tsx)
as the client-side validation gate (CONFIRMED in the earlier `docs/QA0.md` audit, §7: "Client
mirror in `InputForm.submit()`"). This is not a hypothesis — the current shipping frontend
already runs this exact function in the browser on every keystroke-driven submit. The Zod
schemas in `shared/src/contract.ts` are likewise already imported by the frontend today
(`DOC_TYPES`, `DOC_TYPE_LABELS` in `InputForm.tsx`; `GenerationRequestSchema`/`ExportRequestSchema`
would need to be imported too, but Zod itself is a widely-used, already-isomorphic library with
no Node-specific runtime behavior — nothing in `contract.ts` uses a Node API).

### Filename generation

**Definitely browser-compatible.** `sanitizeBase`/`prefixFilename` in `shared/src/naming.ts` are
pure string/regex operations with a type-only import — no Node API used at all.

### DOCX

This is the pivotal investigation, and it was resolved with direct package inspection rather
than assumption:

- [node_modules/docx/package.json](../node_modules/docx/package.json) — the installed version
  (8.5.0) declares itself: `"description": "Easily generate .docx files with JS/TS with a nice
  declarative API. Works for Node and on the Browser."` (CONFIRMED, exact string from the
  installed package).
- The package ships `type: "module"`, with `exports` for both `require` (`index.cjs`) and
  `import` (`index.mjs`), **and** a separate `index.iife.js` build file present in
  `node_modules/docx/build/` — an IIFE bundle is specifically a `<script>`-tag/global-scope
  browser distribution format, not something Node consumers need (CONFIRMED, directory listing).
- Reading the actual shipped type declaration,
  [node_modules/docx/build/export/packer/packer.d.ts](../node_modules/docx/build/export/packer/packer.d.ts),
  confirms the `Packer` class exposes:
  ```
  static toString(file, prettify?): Promise<string>
  static toBuffer(file, prettify?): Promise<Buffer>      ← currently used by buildWord
  static toBase64String(file, prettify?): Promise<string>
  static toBlob(file, prettify?): Promise<Blob>          ← native browser output method
  static toStream(file, prettify?): Stream               ← Node-only (imports "stream")
  ```
  `toBlob` returning a native `Blob` is exactly the same primitive
  [web/src/features/export/ExportControls.tsx](../web/src/features/export/ExportControls.tsx)'s
  `triggerBrowserDownload()` already consumes today for whatever the server currently returns —
  no new download mechanism is needed on the frontend at all.
- **One caveat found, not glossed over**: `docx`'s own `devDependencies` include
  `vite-plugin-node-polyfills` (CONFIRMED, `package.json`). This is the library authors' own
  tooling for their demo/browser build, and is a signal — not proof — that bundling `docx` for
  the browser with Vite may require adding a Node-polyfill plugin (most likely for the `buffer`
  global used internally by `jszip`/`docx` even when the public API returns a `Blob`). This is a
  small, well-known, single-line Vite config addition (`vite-plugin-node-polyfills`), not an
  architectural obstacle — but it was not executed in this session (see limitation note below).
- **Limitation, stated plainly**: no live `vite build`/browser execution of `docx` was performed
  in this session. The current operating constraints for this investigation restrict file writes
  to `docs/`, and a genuine bundle-and-run spike would require creating temporary source files
  outside `docs/` (e.g., a scratch Vite entry point). The evidence above is therefore **static
  package evidence** (type declarations, `package.json`, shipped build artifacts), which is
  strong and specific, but **not yet an executed runtime proof**. This is flagged as the first
  concrete task for the Builder agent (§18), not left as an open question requiring the platform
  team — it is answerable entirely from this repository with a few minutes of `npm install
  vite-plugin-node-polyfills` + a throwaway component.

**Classification: Browser-compatible with a small, well-understood configuration change**
(one API-call swap + likely one Vite plugin), not "probably not browser-compatible" and not
"definitely requires Node."

### PDF

**Browser-compatible with small changes.** `buildPdf`'s entire algorithm (PDF object-graph
string construction, `%PDF-1.4` header, `xref` table, `trailer`) uses no Node API whatsoever
except the single line `Buffer.from(pdf, "utf-8")` at the very end, purely to produce a byte
buffer. Replacing that one line with `new Blob([pdf], { type: "application/pdf" })` is
sufficient — the entire rest of the function is portable unchanged (CONFIRMED by direct reading
of [exportService.ts](../server/src/app/exportService.ts)).

### HTML

**Already essentially browser-compatible.** `buildMockup` builds an HTML string via template
interpolation and manual `&`/`<` escaping, then wraps it in `Buffer.from(html, "utf-8")` only for
the HTTP response step. The string-building logic needs no change at all; only the final
buffer-vs-Blob wrapping differs, exactly as with PDF.

### Downloads

**No new download mechanism needs to be invented.** The current frontend
([web/src/features/export/ExportControls.tsx](../web/src/features/export/ExportControls.tsx))
already implements `triggerBrowserDownload(filename, blob)` using `URL.createObjectURL` and a
synthetic anchor click — this exact function works identically whether the `Blob` came from a
`fetch()` response (today) or was constructed locally in the browser (post-migration). This is
the strongest single piece of evidence that a client-side rebuild is low-risk on the frontend
side: **the download code does not need to change at all**, only where the `Blob` comes from.

---

## 6. Client-Side Architecture Feasibility

Combining §4 and §5: every backend responsibility SpecPilot currently has —
generation (3 functions), validation (1 function + Zod schemas), filename logic (2 functions),
and export (3 functions) — is either already proven running in the browser today, or requires
only a small, well-understood, single-file change (one `Packer.toBuffer`→`toBlob` swap, two
`Buffer.from`→`Blob` swaps, and possibly one Vite polyfill plugin). There is no remaining
generation or export responsibility in this application that structurally requires a server.

This is a **fact pattern specific to this application** (deterministic, side-effect-free,
in-memory template generation with no persistence/auth/external calls — the explicit ADR
decision in [adr/ADR-DETERMINISTIC.md](../adr/ADR-DETERMINISTIC.md)), not a generic claim that
"all backends can move to the browser." A different application with a real database, real
authentication, or genuine external integrations would not have this property.

---

## 7. XYZ `server.mjs` / Backend Hosting Feasibility

No new information exists beyond what `docs/WhatIFoundAboutXYZ.md` already states (re-checked;
no other XYZ source is available). Restating precisely, with the CONFIRMED/INFERRED/UNKNOWN
distinctions unchanged from `MigrationAnalysis0.md` §9, because nothing has changed here:

- **CONFIRMED**: `server.mjs` is documented to serve static files, proxy `/_api/*` to
  `BACKEND_URL`, and optionally inject an OAuth token — a fixed, named responsibility set.
- **CONFIRMED**: the template's own README instructs adding new API functions to
  `app/src/api/client.ts` (the frontend Axios client), not to `server.mjs` — every documented
  customization point for API behavior is on the frontend side of the proxy, not the server side.
- **INFERRED, not confirmed**: this suggests `server.mjs` is intended to be used largely as
  platform-owned infrastructure with a fixed contract, and that application-specific backend
  logic is expected to live in a separate, already-existing `BACKEND_URL` service — but the
  document never explicitly states "you may/may not add custom Express routes to `server.mjs`,"
  so this remains an inference, not a fact.
- **UNKNOWN**: whether `server.mjs` can be edited/extended by an application team at all.

Given this remains unresolved and unresolvable from available material, and given that
Architecture B (§6) removes the need to answer it, this document treats Q1 from
`MigrationAnalysis0.md` as **de-prioritized, not eliminated** — it would only need answering if
future requirements reintroduce a genuine server-side need (e.g., a real external
integration).

---

## 8. Architecture A — Full-Stack XYZ Application

Frontend + backend (extended `server.mjs` or a bespoke Node process) colocated in the XYZ app.

- **Feasibility**: unresolved — entirely gated on the unanswered `server.mjs` extensibility
  question (§7). Cannot be executed with confidence today.
- **Required changes**: directory restructuring into `app/src`, plus either extending
  `server.mjs` with `/health`, `/api/generate`, `/api/export` (or their `/_api` equivalents), or
  standing up SpecPilot's Express app as a separately-hosted `BACKEND_URL` target (itself a new
  deployment surface, outside the scope of "one XYZ app").
- **XYZ compatibility**: UNKNOWN, blocked on Q1.
- **Deployment complexity**: HIGH relative to B — either a modified platform-owned file (risk of
  drifting from supported patterns) or an entirely separate backend deployment to operate and
  secure.
- **Maintenance**: MEDIUM — inherits the current dual-validation-layer duplication and the
  pre-existing static-serving gap unless deliberately fixed during the port.
- **Testing**: MEDIUM — HTTP-route-level tests (`tests/http/*.test.ts`) remain relevant largely
  unchanged, but only if the routes can be hosted at all.
- **Runtime complexity**: MEDIUM-HIGH — an additional process/service boundary (proxy + backend)
  where none is functionally necessary for this application's actual behavior.
- **Future development**: MEDIUM — every new document type or export format still requires
  backend deployment coordination.
- **Risks**: HIGH — the entire option is contingent on an unanswered platform question; building
  toward it risks wasted effort if the answer is "no."

## 9. Architecture B — Pure Frontend XYZ Application

Frontend-only; generation and export run entirely in the browser; no `BACKEND_URL`, no OAuth, no
proxy configuration needed at all.

- **Feasibility**: HIGH — grounded in §4–§6's direct evidence, not speculation.
- **Required changes**: relocate `prdGen`/`trsGen`/`uxGen`/`validate`/`naming`/`contract` into
  the frontend source tree unchanged; modify `exportService.ts`'s three functions to return
  `Blob` instead of `{filename, contentType, buffer}` (one API swap for Word, one `Blob`
  substitution each for PDF/HTML); wire these directly into `ExportControls`'s existing
  `triggerBrowserDownload` path instead of via `fetch("/api/export")`.
- **Browser compatibility**: HIGH confidence from static package evidence (§5); one
  configuration item (Vite Node-polyfill plugin) plausible but unverified by live execution
  (flagged, §18).
- **Bundle size**: not measured in this session (no build was run); `docx`'s dependency footprint
  (`jszip`, `nanoid`, `xml`, `xml-js`) is moderate but standard for a document-generation library
  — this should be measured with an actual `vite build` as an early Builder task, not assumed.
- **Performance**: SpecPilot's own PDF exporter already caps at 50 lines of content (a
  pre-existing limitation, `docs/QA0.md` §8.9) and the Word export uses one `Paragraph` per input
  line — both are small, bounded, synchronous, single-document operations well within normal
  browser main-thread capability for typical product-description-length input; no batch/bulk or
  concurrent-generation requirement exists in this application (CONFIRMED — one document
  generated/exported at a time, per the current UI).
- **Security/data handling** (§ per your explicit question, addressed directly): the current
  application already has no authentication, no database, and no external API calls (CONFIRMED,
  [adr/ADR-DETERMINISTIC.md](../adr/ADR-DETERMINISTIC.md), [Spec.md](../Spec.md) Non-Goals).
  Moving generation/export to the browser does not expose user input to any **additional**
  system — today, the user's product title/details already leave the browser only to reach
  SpecPilot's own same-origin Express server and back; under Architecture B, that data never
  leaves the browser at all. This is a **reduction**, not an increase, in data exposure, and
  there is no security/policy reason found in this repository requiring server-side processing
  (no audit-logging requirement, no server-side data validation against a trusted store, no
  business logic requiring secrecy). This is stated as a genuine advantage of Architecture B, not
  an assumption.
- **Maintainability**: HIGH — removes the entire Express/HTTP layer, the dual-validation-layer
  duplication, and the pre-existing static-serving defect, since there is no longer a static
  file to fail to serve (XYZ/whatever host serves the SPA directly).
- **XYZ compatibility**: HIGH — a pure static frontend is the simplest possible shape for the
  XYZ template described in `docs/WhatIFoundAboutXYZ.md`; it sidesteps `BACKEND_URL`, OAuth,
  and the entire `server.mjs` extensibility question.
- **Deployment simplicity**: HIGH — no backend to deploy, secure, or monitor at all.
- **Testing**: HIGH — the existing `tests/core/*.test.ts` and the relevant assertions in
  `tests/app/exportService.test.ts` are already written against pure functions and should port to
  a browser/jsdom test target with minimal changes (return-type assertions on `Blob` instead of
  `Buffer`/`ExportedFile`).
- **Risks**: LOW-MEDIUM — the only unresolved item is the unexecuted browser-bundle spike for
  `docx` (§5), which is answerable within this repository without any platform dependency.

## 10. Architecture C — Separate Backend + XYZ Frontend

Included because it is a meaningful possibility raised implicitly by the `BACKEND_URL` proxy
model in `docs/WhatIFoundAboutXYZ.md`: SpecPilot's current Express app could be deployed as an
independent service (anywhere — not necessarily "in XYZ" at all, since
`docs/WhatIFoundAboutXYZ.md` never states backend services must themselves be XYZ
applications) and referenced via `BACKEND_URL` from a XYZ-hosted frontend.

- **Feasibility**: technically straightforward (the current `server/` code already runs
  standalone via `npm run dev:server` and the existing `Dockerfile`), but it reintroduces a
  second deployment surface, a second set of operational concerns (uptime, scaling, its own
  health checks), and the `/_api` proxy + OAuth configuration question (§7) for a backend that,
  per §6, does not need to exist at all for this application's actual requirements.
- **When this would make sense**: only if Architecture B's browser-execution spike (§18) fails
  unexpectedly (e.g., `docx` genuinely cannot be bundled for the browser even with polyfills) —
  in that case, Architecture C becomes the fallback rather than Architecture A, because it avoids
  the unresolved `server.mjs`-extensibility question entirely by not needing `server.mjs` to host
  anything beyond its documented proxy role.
- **Not recommended as the primary path**, because it carries real operational cost (a second
  deployable, its own CI/CD, its own scaling/monitoring) to solve a problem (§6) that appears not
  to exist for this application.

---

## 11. Migration vs Rebuild

- **Migrate/refactor** (carry forward `server/`'s Express app, adapt it to fit XYZ): rejected
  as the primary path — not because it's infeasible in principle, but because it depends on an
  unanswered platform question (Q1) and, even if answered favorably, would carry forward two
  confirmed pre-existing defects (dual validation layers, missing static-file serving) for no
  functional benefit, per the same reasoning as `MigrationAnalysis0.md` §14.
- **Rebuild** (as recommended, Architecture B): this is explicitly a **relocation of already-pure
  logic**, not a rewrite of business rules. The generator functions, validation function, and
  filename logic move with **zero logic changes** (only their execution location changes, from
  server-received-request to browser-triggered-action). The three exporter functions move with
  **minimal, mechanical changes** (one API swap, two `Blob` substitutions). This materially lowers
  the risk normally associated with "rebuild from scratch," because the actual behavior being
  reproduced is not being reinvented — it is being copied.
- Given SpecPilot's small size and fully deterministic nature (explicitly acknowledged by you as
  a reason you're willing to invest rebuild effort), this is a case where "rebuild" and "low
  risk" are not in tension.

---

## 12. Decision Tree

```
Is docx's Packer.toBlob() confirmed to work when bundled by Vite for the browser? (small spike needed, §18)
├── YES (expected outcome, based on static package evidence in §5)
│     └── Architecture B (pure frontend) — RECOMMENDED, no platform question needed at all.
├── YES, but only with a Node-polyfill Vite plugin
│     └── Still Architecture B — polyfill plugin is a known, contained, one-line addition.
└── NO (docx cannot be bundled for the browser even with polyfills — contradicts current static evidence)
      └── Word export alone must remain server-side; PDF/HTML/generation/validation still move to the browser.
            ├── Is "server.mjs" extensible with a single custom Word-export route? (Q1, platform question)
            │     ├── YES → Architecture A, but scoped to ONLY the Word export route (minimal backend surface)
            │     └── NO/UNKNOWN → Architecture C (separate small Word-export service), OR drop
            │           native .docx export and ship Word content as HTML/RTF instead (a product
            │           decision, not an architectural one — noted here, not decided here)
```

The tree collapses to a single recommended branch (Architecture B) unless the one flagged spike
(§18) produces a negative result — which current static evidence makes unlikely, but which has
not been executed.

---

## 13. Recommended Target Architecture

**Architecture B — pure frontend, browser-executed generation and export**, hosted as a static
XYZ application with no backend, no `BACKEND_URL`, and no OAuth configuration.

This is a decisive recommendation, not a conditional one, subject only to the single remaining
spike in §18 (which is answerable within this repository, without platform input).

---

## 14. If We Rebuild: Exactly What Should Be Built

**Application type**: a pure static single-page React application. No server-side component of
any kind ships with the application itself.

**Directory structure** (targeting the XYZ template's `app/src/` convention from
`docs/WhatIFoundAboutXYZ.md`):

```
app/
  src/
    main.tsx                  # entry point (unchanged pattern from web/src/main.tsx)
    App.tsx                   # composition root (unchanged pattern)
    generation/
      contract.ts             # from shared/src/contract.ts — GenerationRequest, GeneratedDocument, DocType, DOC_TYPES, etc.
      validate.ts             # from shared/src/validate.ts — unchanged
      naming.ts                # from shared/src/naming.ts — unchanged
      prdGen.ts                # from server/src/core/prdGen.ts — unchanged
      trsGen.ts                # from server/src/core/trsGen.ts — unchanged
      uxGen.ts                 # from server/src/core/uxGen.ts — unchanged
      genService.ts             # from server/src/app/genService.ts — same selection/ordering logic, no HTTP wrapping
    export/
      exportService.ts          # from server/src/app/exportService.ts — buildWord/buildPdf/buildMockup, modified to return Blob
    app/
      AppShell.tsx, HelpPanel.tsx   # unchanged from web/src/app/**
    features/
      input/InputForm.tsx           # unchanged (already calls validate() client-side)
      output/OutputView.tsx         # unchanged
      export/ExportControls.tsx     # modified: calls exportService directly instead of fetch("/api/export")
    theme/
      ThemeProvider.tsx, tokens.ts   # unchanged
```

**Frontend architecture**: identical component tree to today (`AppShell` → `InputForm` +
`OutputView` + `ExportControls`), same local `useState`-only state management — no new state
library needed.

**Runtime architecture**: none — no server process ships with the application; the app is
static HTML/JS/CSS served by whatever XYZ's static-hosting mechanism turns out to be.

**API requirements**: none. `InputForm`'s "Generate" button calls `genService.generate(request)`
directly (an in-browser function call, not a `fetch`); `ExportControls`'s buttons call
`exportService.buildExport(format, content, title, docType)` directly and receive a `Blob`
immediately, reusing the existing `triggerBrowserDownload` unchanged.

**Where generation logic lives**: `app/src/generation/` (see tree above) — copied near-verbatim
from `server/src/core/**` and `server/src/app/genService.ts`.

**Where export logic lives**: `app/src/export/exportService.ts` — copied from
`server/src/app/exportService.ts` with three specific changes: `Packer.toBuffer` → `Packer.toBlob`
in `buildWord`; `Buffer.from(pdf, "utf-8")` → `new Blob([pdf], {type:"application/pdf"})` in
`buildPdf`; `Buffer.from(html, "utf-8")` → `new Blob([html], {type:"text/html"})` in `buildMockup`.

**Dependency requirements**: `docx`, `zod`, `react`, `react-dom` (all already public npm
packages, per `docs/MigrationAnalysis0.md` §8); likely `vite-plugin-node-polyfills` as a new
devDependency, pending the §18 spike; **`express` is no longer needed at all**.

**Configuration requirements**: none beyond what the XYZ template's frontend build already
expects — no `BACKEND_URL`, no `OAUTH_*` variables, since there is no proxy target.

**XYZ integration points**: purely as a static build artifact (`dist/`) — no custom
`server.mjs` behavior is required, which is precisely why this architecture avoids the
unresolved Q1 (§7) entirely.

**Testing strategy**: port `tests/core/*.test.ts`, `tests/shared/*.test.ts`, and the relevant
assertions from `tests/app/exportService.test.ts` to run under Vitest with a `jsdom` (or
browser-mode) environment instead of Node, asserting `Blob` outputs where the current tests
assert `Buffer`/`ExportedFile`. `tests/web/*.tsx` component tests port with minimal change since
`ExportControls`'s external contract (`onDownload(filename, blob)`) does not change at all.

---

## 15. If We Migrate: Exactly What Should Change

(Provided for completeness, in case the §18 spike produces an unexpected negative result and
Architecture A must be reconsidered — not the recommended path.)

- Relocate `web/src` + `shared/src` into `app/src` unchanged.
- Contingent entirely on Q1 (§7) being answered "yes": extend the XYZ `server.mjs` with three
  routes matching `server/src/http/app.ts`'s current handlers (`/health`, `/api/generate`,
  `/api/export`, or their `/_api`-prefixed equivalents), reusing `genService.ts` and
  `exportService.ts` unchanged (Node-hosted, so no `Blob` conversion needed).
- Fix the pre-existing static-serving gap and dual-validation-layer duplication as part of the
  same move (both already identified as defects independent of XYZ).
- This path cannot be planned in further concrete detail until Q1 is answered — attempting to do
  so would repeat the same conditional-recommendation problem `MigrationAnalysis0.md` already
  flagged.

---

## 16. Remaining Platform Questions

Prioritized — only what remains genuinely necessary after this investigation:

1. **(Low priority under the recommended architecture, but worth confirming for the record)**
   *"Does the XYZ static-hosting/build pipeline accept a pure frontend application with no
   configured `BACKEND_URL` and no OAuth variables at all, or is a backend target mandatory
   infrastructure regardless of whether the application needs one?"*
   *Why it matters*: confirms Architecture B is a normal supported shape, not a workaround.
   *Decision depending on it*: whether any placeholder/no-op backend configuration is still
   required for deployment to succeed, even if unused.

2. **(Only relevant if §18's spike fails)** *"Is `server.mjs` an application-owned entrypoint
   that teams may modify to register custom Express routes, or is it a fixed XYZ runtime
   component limited to serving `dist/` and proxying `/_api/*` to `BACKEND_URL`?"*
   *Why it matters*: only becomes decision-relevant if Word export cannot be moved to the
   browser after all.
   *Decision depending on it*: Architecture A (scoped to Word-export-only) vs. Architecture C.

All other questions raised in `docs/MigrationAnalysis0.md` §11 (Node version, `PORT` injection,
health-check contract, GitHub Packages) are **no longer load-bearing** for the recommended
architecture, since a pure static frontend has no runtime process for XYZ to start, version,
port-bind, or health-check in the way those questions assumed.

---

## 17. Risks

- **Primary risk**: the `docx`-in-browser bundling spike (§18) has not been executed; if it
  reveals a genuine blocker beyond a polyfill plugin, Word export specifically (not the whole
  application) would need to fall back to Architecture A (scoped) or C.
- **Secondary risk**: bundle size/first-load performance of a browser bundle containing `docx` +
  `jszip` has not been measured; if unacceptable, Word export could be deferred to a background
  Web Worker (a refinement, not an architectural change) rather than reverting to a server.
- **Low risk**: the existing PDF exporter's pre-existing 50-line truncation (`docs/QA0.md` §8.9)
  carries forward unchanged in Architecture B — this is a pre-existing product limitation, not
  introduced by the migration, and should be tracked separately regardless of architecture chosen.
- **Low risk**: removing the backend also removes the current dual-validation-layer duplication
  by construction (there is no longer a second server-side gate) — a net simplification, not a
  new risk.

---

## 18. Validation Plan

1. **Spike (first Builder task, not part of this planning document)**: create a throwaway Vite
   entry that imports `docx`, constructs a trivial `Document`, calls `Packer.toBlob()`, and
   triggers a browser download — confirm it builds and the resulting `.docx` opens correctly.
   Add `vite-plugin-node-polyfills` only if the unmodified build fails with a missing-global
   error (e.g., `Buffer is not defined`).
2. Port `tests/core/prdGen.test.ts`/`trsGen.test.ts`/`uxGen.test.ts` unchanged (they assert pure
   string content, independent of runtime environment).
3. Port `tests/app/exportService.test.ts`, changing only the assertions on the returned artifact
   type (`Blob` instead of `Buffer`/`ExportedFile`), keeping the filename/content-type assertions
   identical.
4. Re-run the equivalent of `tests/web/exportControls.test.tsx` against the new
   directly-in-browser `exportService`, replacing the `stubFetchBlob()` mock with a direct call
   to the real (now client-side) `buildExport`.
5. Manually verify no behavior regresses relative to `docs/QA0.md` §12 "Must Not Break": same
   section ordering/content, same filename convention, same "only selected types generated"
   guarantee.

---

## 19. Final Recommendation

**Recreate SpecPilot as a pure frontend application (Architecture B).** Move
`prdGen`/`trsGen`/`uxGen`/`validate`/`naming`/`contract` into the browser unchanged, and modify
`exportService.ts`'s three functions to produce `Blob` output instead of a Node `Buffer`/HTTP
response — a one-line change for PDF and HTML, and a swap to the `docx` package's own
already-shipped `Packer.toBlob()` browser API for Word. This eliminates the Express backend, the
current Docker/deployment setup, and the entire unresolved "can `server.mjs` host custom
routes" question in one step, because there is no longer a backend that needs hosting. The only
remaining unresolved item is a small, repository-internal technical spike (§18) — not a platform
question — making this the first thing the Builder agent should verify before beginning the
rebuild.

1. Start from the XYZ application structure rather than trying to preserve the old Express deployment model.

   Word:

   PDF:
     Buffer.from(...) -> Blob

   HTML:
     Buffer.from(...) -> Blob

   The export functions should return a useful browser artifact such as:

     { filename, blob, contentType }

   or another clean equivalent that fits the existing frontend architecture.

6. Remove the fetch-based export path.

   ExportControls should call the client-side export service directly rather than:

     fetch("/api/export")

7. Reuse the existing browser download mechanism wherever possible:
   URL.createObjectURL(...)
   anchor.click()
   URL.revokeObjectURL(...)

8. Do not introduce a state-management library unless there is an actual need.

9. Do not introduce a backend merely because the old application had one.

10. Do not introduce unnecessary abstractions.

11. Do not change generated document semantics merely to make the implementation prettier.

Port/adapt the existing tests rather than throwing them away.

- PRD generation
- TRS generation
- UX generation
- validation
- filename generation
- document-type selection

- PDF export

- the existing "Must Not Break" requirements from QA0.md

IMPORTANT BEHAVIORAL REQUIREMENT

The rebuild must not silently change product behavior.

Treat the existing application as the behavioral reference.

If you discover an existing defect while rebuilding, distinguish between:

A. behavior that must be preserved for compatibility
B. an obvious bug that should be fixed
C. an architectural artifact that should disappear

Do not mix those categories.

ARCHITECTURAL CLEANUP IS ENCOURAGED

You are explicitly allowed to remove things that only existed because of the old backend architecture, including:

- Express
- HTTP generation routes
- HTTP export routes
- server-side request wrappers
- BACKEND_URL configuration
- OAuth configuration that is only relevant to the backend
- Docker/server deployment machinery
- duplicated server/client validation

But only remove something after confirming it is genuinely unused by the final application.

XYZ COMPATIBILITY

Use the XYZ template's actual conventions from the repository/documentation.

Do not invent unsupported XYZ behavior.

The target should be the simplest supported XYZ application shape:

static frontend + normal XYZ build/deployment.

There should be no dependency on custom server.mjs routes.

BE OPEN TO A DIFFERENT REBUILD IF WARRANTED

Although Architecture B is the current recommendation, don't treat the proposed directory tree as sacred.

If, during implementation, you find that the cleanest XYZ-native solution is to reorganize the application differently, that is acceptable.

Likewise, if you discover evidence that contradicts the planning report, stop and reassess rather than forcing the predetermined architecture.

The governing goal is:

"Build the smallest, cleanest, most reliable XYZ-native version of SpecPilot that preserves the existing product behavior."

Do not optimize for preserving the old repository structure.

DO NOT ADD FEATURES

This is an architectural rebuild, not a feature-development exercise.

Do not add:
- authentication
- database
- cloud storage
- analytics
- external APIs
- new document types
- new UI workflows
- speculative abstractions

unless the existing application already requires them.

14. Credit strategy
I checked GitHub's current documentation because the Copilot billing/model situation has changed in 2026.

GitHub now describes usage in terms of AI credits and token consumption, rather than the old simple premium-request model. One AI credit is $0.01, and model cost depends on input/output/cached tokens. 
G
GitHub Docs
+1

So the old "X premium requests" comparisons you may find online aren't necessarily applicable to your account anymore. GitHub explicitly says the June 1, 2026 transition moved Copilot to usage-based billing. 
G
GitHub Docs

That makes model choice + context size + number of agent turns important.

And I don't actually see the image containing your model choices in the message I received, so I can't reliably rank the exact models in your screenshot. If you upload it again, I can compare every model shown.

But based on the currently documented models, here's how I'd approach this.

ChatGPT said:
Yes — install the .msi. That's the easiest and correct choice for your setup.

Choose:

Windows Installer (.msi) → x64

Why .msi instead of .zip?
.msi = normal Windows installer. It installs Node.js, npm, and adds Node to your PATH automatically.
.zip = portable/manual installation. You'd have to configure PATH yourself, so there's no advantage for you here.
So download:

Node.js v22.23.2 LTS → Windows Installer (.msi) → x64

Then install it with the default options.

After installation, close and reopen PowerShell and run:

node --version
npm --version









If you get:

v22.23.2
10.9.8









you're good.

ChatGPT said:
Yes. Now that I have both trees, we can be much more precise.

And I agree with your approach: we should prepare the XYZ repo ourselves first, with a deliberate file-by-file mapping, and only then hand it to Builder. Builder should not have to infer where the old files came from or reconstruct the migration history.

The key principle is:

XYZ remains the host/application skeleton. SpecPilot contributes functionality, UI, tests, and documentation. We do not transplant the old repository wholesale.

That avoids exactly the merge/confusion problem you were concerned about.

1. The final mental model
Think of the two repositories like this:

OLD SPECpilot REPO                         NEW XYZ REPO
=================                         ===============

web/src/              ───────────────►    app/src/
server/src/core/      ───────────────►    app/src/generation/
server/src/app/       ───────────────►    app/src/generation/ + app/src/export/
shared/src/            ───────────────►   app/src/generation/

tests/                 ───────────────►   app/tests/  (or XYZ's test location)
docs/                  ───────────────►   docs/
adr/                   ───────────────►   docs/adr/
.github/               ───────────────►   .github/

Docker / Express       ───────────────►   NOTHING
old Vite config        ───────────────►   NOTHING
old package.json       ───────────────►   NOTHING
old package-lock       ───────────────►   NOTHING
old dist               ───────────────►   NOTHING
old deploy             ───────────────►   NOTHING









The XYZ infrastructure remains XYZ infrastructure.

2. What you should copy immediately
I'd make the following changes to the new XYZ repository, before Builder touches it.

XYZ root
Eventually:

stla.gadp.docker.react.template/
├── .github/
├── docs/                    ← CREATE
├── app/
├── deployment/
├── docker/
├── config/
├── docker-bake.hcl
├── .dockerignore
├── .gitignore
└── README.md









Don't put SpecPilot's documentation inside app/.

Your instinct there is correct.

if the design document is useful.

The .html files can be left in the old repo unless you specifically want them archived. They aren't part of the XYZ application.

5. .github — merge, don't replace
This one is important.

XYZ already has:

.github/
└── workflows/
    ├── docker-bake-ci.yaml
    └── re-seal-sealed-secrets.yaml









Your SpecPilot repo has:

.github/
├── copilot-instructions.md
└── agents/
    ├── auditor.agent.md
    ├── builder.agent.md
    └── planner.agent.md









The correct result is:

XYZ/
└── .github/
    ├── copilot-instructions.md
    ├── agents/
    │   ├── auditor.agent.md
    │   ├── builder.agent.md
    │   └── planner.agent.md
    │
    └── workflows/
        ├── docker-bake-ci.yaml
        └── re-seal-sealed-secrets.yaml









Do not replace XYZ .github with the old .github.

There is no conflict between these particular files, so this is straightforward.

One caveat: your Copilot instructions were written for the old repository architecture. Since Builder won't have the old repo available, we'll eventually need to update them to reflect the new XYZ paths.

But don't rewrite them yet. First establish the physical migration structure. Then we'll make a dedicated migration/integration document and update the instructions coherently.

6. The most important part: web/src
This is where we need to be careful.

Old:

web/src/
├── App.tsx
├── main.tsx
├── api/
│   └── client.ts
├── app/
│   ├── AppShell.tsx
│   └── HelpPanel.tsx

│   │   └── ExportControls.tsx
│   ├── input/

│   └── output/
│       └── OutputView.tsx
└── theme/
    ├── ThemeProvider.tsx
    └── tokens.ts









XYZ already has:

app/src/
├── main.tsx
├── App.tsx
├── App.css
├── api/
│   └── client.ts
├── routes/
│   ├── Home.tsx
│   └── ApiExample.tsx
└── vite-env.d.ts









Therefore:

Do NOT copy web/src over app/src.
That would be the wrong move.

Instead, take the functional components from the old app and place them into the XYZ app.

The target should become approximately:

app/src/
├── main.tsx
├── App.tsx
├── App.css
│
├── api/
│   └── client.ts
│
├── app/
│   ├── AppShell.tsx
│   └── HelpPanel.tsx
│
├── features/
│   ├── export/
│   │   └── ExportControls.tsx
│   ├── input/
│   │   └── InputForm.tsx
│   └── output/
│       └── OutputView.tsx
│
├── generation/
│   ├── contract.ts
│   ├── validate.ts
│   ├── naming.ts
│   ├── prdGen.ts
│   ├── trsGen.ts
│   ├── uxGen.ts
│   └── genService.ts
│
├── export/
│   └── exportService.ts
│
├── theme/
│   ├── ThemeProvider.tsx
│   └── tokens.ts
│
└── vite-env.d.ts









This is the structure I would target.

7. What happens to main.tsx and App.tsx?
These are not copied blindly.

Old:

web/src/main.tsx
web/src/App.tsx









XYZ already has:

app/src/main.tsx
app/src/App.tsx









Therefore:

XYZ's files win as the starting point.

The SpecPilot application composition gets integrated into them.

The final App.tsx should become the SpecPilot application within the XYZ shell rather than preserving the XYZ demo routes.

For example, conceptually:

XYZ App
    ↓
SpecPilot UI
    ├── AppShell
    ├── InputForm
    ├── OutputView
    └── ExportControls









The XYZ example pages:

routes/Home.tsx
routes/ApiExample.tsx









are likely temporary template/demo functionality.

We should not automatically delete them before understanding the XYZ template, but they are not part of the SpecPilot product.

Eventually they will probably disappear.

8. What happens to api/client.ts?
This is an important architectural difference.

Old SpecPilot:

web/src/api/client.ts









talks to:

/api/generate
/api/export









XYZ:

app/src/api/client.ts









is designed around:

/_api/*









But our target Architecture B has:

No application API at all.

Therefore, the old SpecPilot API client is not something we should preserve as part of the final architecture.

I would initially leave XYZ's api/client.ts untouched.

Then, once the application is integrated, Builder can remove the API usage because:

InputForm
    ↓
genService.generate()
    ↓
browser

ExportControls
    ↓
exportService.buildExport()
    ↓
Blob
    ↓
download









No:

fetch()
    ↓
Express
    ↓
HTTP









So do not copy the old api/client.ts over XYZ's one.

9. Generation logic
This part is much cleaner.

Take:

server/src/core/
├── prdGen.ts
├── trsGen.ts
└── uxGen.ts









and put them here:

app/src/generation/
├── prdGen.ts
├── trsGen.ts
└── uxGen.ts









These are essentially pure functions and the investigation already established that they have no meaningful Node runtime dependency.

10. Shared code
Take:

shared/src/
├── contract.ts
├── naming.ts
└── validate.ts









and put them here:

app/src/generation/
├── contract.ts
├── naming.ts
└── validate.ts









I'd not create a shared/ directory inside XYZ.

Why?

Because there is no longer a frontend/backend split.

Previously:

shared
  ↑
web       server









made architectural sense.

Now:

app/src/
    generation/









is the natural home.

This is one of the benefits of eliminating the backend.

11. shared/src/index.ts
This one is probably not needed.

The old:

shared/src/index.ts









exists to expose the shared package/module.

Once these files live directly inside:

app/src/generation/









we can use direct imports.

For example:

import { validate } from "../generation/validate";









rather than maintaining a separate shared package boundary.

So:

Do not copy shared/src/index.ts unless the actual imports require it.

12. genService.ts
Move:

server/src/app/genService.ts









to:

app/src/generation/genService.ts









This is important.

It becomes the browser-side orchestration layer:

InputForm
    ↓
genService
    ├── prdGen
    ├── trsGen
    └── uxGen









No HTTP.

No Express.

No server request/response.

13. exportService.ts
Move:

server/src/app/exportService.ts









to:

app/src/export/exportService.ts









But this is the one file we know will need architectural modification.

The old version effectively does:

buildWord()
    ↓
Buffer

buildPdf()
    ↓
Buffer

buildMockup()
    ↓
Buffer









The new version should do:

buildWord()
    ↓
Blob

buildPdf()
    ↓
Blob

buildMockup()
    ↓
Blob









And:

ExportControls
    ↓
buildExport()
    ↓
Blob
    ↓
triggerBrowserDownload()









So copy it to the new location as source material, but don't manually rewrite it before Builder/the spike.

14. The HTTP server files — do NOT move them
These:

server/src/index.ts
server/src/http/app.ts
server/src/http/errors.ts









should not be copied into XYZ.

They belong to the old architecture.

Likewise, do not copy:

server/









as a directory.

The final application shouldn't contain:

server/









at all.

15. The old Dockerfile — definitely don't copy
Don't move:

SpecPilot-main/Dockerfile









The XYZ repository already has its own:

docker/
├── node20.11/
│   ├── Dockerfile
│   └── docker-entrypoint.sh









XYZ owns deployment infrastructure.

The old Dockerfile belongs to the old architecture and would just create confusion.

16. Old deploy/ — don't move
Don't copy:

deploy/
├── README.md
└── smoke.sh









XYZ already has:

deployment/









with its own Kubernetes/Helm deployment structure.

Again:

XYZ deployment infrastructure stays XYZ infrastructure.

If some information in deploy/README.md is important, put that information into the appropriate documentation later.

Don't transplant the deployment scripts.

17. Old dist/ — absolutely don't move
Do not copy:

dist/









It's generated output.

The XYZ build will generate its own output.

18. coverage/ — don't move
Don't copy:

coverage/









It's generated test output.

19. scripts/coverage-report.mjs
I would not move this initially.

The XYZ project has its own CI/build infrastructure.

If we later discover that the coverage report script is useful and compatible, we can intentionally integrate it.

Not part of the initial migration.

20. Tests
This is the one area where I don't want you manually dumping files somewhere arbitrary until we've seen the XYZ testing configuration.

Your old tests are valuable:

tests/
├── app/
├── build/
├── core/
├── deploy/
├── docs/
├── e2e/
├── http/
├── shared/
└── web/









But some are now obsolete.

Definitely valuable
These test the actual application logic:

tests/core/
├── prdGen.test.ts
├── trsGen.test.ts
└── uxGen.test.ts









and:

tests/shared/
├── contract.test.ts
├── naming.test.ts
└── validate.test.ts









and:

tests/app/
├── exportService.test.ts
└── genService.test.ts









These should be carried into the new project.

Tests that become obsolete
These:

tests/http/
├── export.test.ts
└── generate.test.ts









are testing the backend HTTP layer.

Architecture B eliminates that layer.

So they should not be copied as-is.

Their useful behavioral assertions should be preserved through the new direct-function tests.

Similarly:

tests/deploy/smoke.test.ts









is tied to the old deployment architecture.

Don't copy it as-is.

21. Web/component tests
These are valuable:

tests/web/
├── app.test.tsx
├── appShell.test.tsx
├── exportControls.test.tsx
├── inputForm.test.tsx
├── outputView.test.tsx
└── theme.test.tsx









The:

client.test.ts









test needs special treatment because the API client is going away.

So:

tests/web/client.test.ts









should not simply be carried forward.

The component tests should be preserved/adapted.

22. E2E test
This:

tests/e2e/acceptance.test.ts









is potentially very valuable.

I would preserve it as a reference and eventually adapt it to the XYZ application.

It gives us a way to verify:

enter product
    ↓
select PRD/TRS/UX
    ↓
Generate
    ↓
documents appear
    ↓
export









That's exactly what we want to prove after the rebuild.

23. Test setup
This:

tests/setup.ts









is also useful, but should be integrated according to XYZ's existing test configuration.

Again, don't replace XYZ configuration with the old vitest.config.ts.

The XYZ project gets to keep its configuration.

We bring over the tests and adapt them to the XYZ test environment.

24. package.json — VERY IMPORTANT
Do not copy:

SpecPilot-main/package.json









over:

XYZ/app/package.json









This would be one of the worst things we could do.

XYZ's package configuration is the base.

We add whatever SpecPilot dependencies are actually required.

Based on the investigation, the important application dependencies are:

docx
zod









React/Vite/etc. are already XYZ territory.

For testing, whatever XYZ already uses should be retained and then supplemented as needed.

vite-plugin-node-polyfills
Do not install this yet.

The Planner specifically identified it as a possibility for DOCX.

First perform the browser bundling spike.

If the spike proves it is required:

vite-plugin-node-polyfills









gets added deliberately.

If it isn't required, we don't add it.

That's both cleaner and credit-efficient.

25. package-lock.json
Do not copy the old lockfile.

Use:

XYZ/app/package-lock.json









as the authoritative lockfile.

When we add:

docx

we let npm update the XYZ lockfile.

26. Old Vite configuration
Do not copy:

SpecPilot-main/vite.config.ts









XYZ has:

app/vite.config.ts









and its Vite configuration has XYZ-specific behavior.

We modify XYZ's configuration only when necessary.

This is particularly important for the DOCX browser spike.

27. Old TypeScript configuration
Same principle.

Don't copy:

tsconfig.json









from the old repository.

XYZ has:

app/tsconfig.json
app/tsconfig.app.json









Those stay authoritative.

If the migrated code produces TypeScript problems, we adapt the code/imports to XYZ's configuration first.

Only change XYZ config if there's a genuine requirement.

28. index.html
Don't copy the old:

index.html









XYZ already has:

app/index.html









and specifically has its environment configuration mechanism.

Keep XYZ's.

and determine whether XYZ already has Vitest, Jest, browser testing, etc.

The important thing is that we preserve the valuable tests, rather than blindly transplanting the old test architecture.

33. One subtle but important point
You said:

"I could just move folders altogether into XYZ app."

Mostly yes, but not quite.

These are safe-ish folder-level moves:

web/src/app
web/src/features
web/src/theme









and:

server/src/core









and:

shared/src









can be moved as a group only if we then put them under the final intended generation/ structure.

But don't do:

XYZ/app/
    web/
    server/
    shared/









That would preserve the old architecture inside XYZ and make Builder's job harder.

We want:

XYZ/app/src/
    features/
    generation/
    export/
    theme/
    app/









from day one.

Before Builder:

❌ Don't delete XYZ routes/.
❌ Don't delete XYZ server.mjs.
❌ Don't delete XYZ API client.
❌ Don't modify XYZ Docker.
❌ Don't modify XYZ deployment.
❌ Don't install random dependencies.
❌ Don't install vite-plugin-node-polyfills yet.
❌ Don't modify package.json yet unless necessary to install the known runtime dependencies.
❌ Don't rewrite App.tsx.
❌ Don't rewrite exportService.ts.
❌ Don't modify the old repo.
❌ Don't run a giant Builder migration prompt.
We are preparing the workspace first.

app/package.json

It should explicitly say "this file used to be X; it is now Y; these files were intentionally discarded; these are the behavioral invariants." That will become one of Builder's most important pieces of context.

You said:

Hey got the XYZ template working locally (without my code) and the local app working as well. Now can I go ahead with moving the folders and files as you had mentioned?:
You’re right: this repository is a frontend template and does not include a backend API. Read

Read 

Yes, it is working. This confirms the React frontend is running:

```text
VITE ready
Local: http://localhost:3001/
```

The errors only appear because this template contains example API calls, but no backend API.

### Which server is which?

- `npm run dev` starts the **React/Vite development server** on port `3001`.
- `server.mjs` is the **production frontend server**.
- Neither one implements `/health` or `/items`.
- A backend URL would come from a separate API project or service provided by your team.

You can now add your frontend code under `src`. You do not need a backend URL to build the UI.

### Run the frontend

From `app`:

```powershell
npm run dev
```

Then open:

```text
http://localhost:3001
```

### About the health errors

The example code calls:

```text
/_api/health
/_api/items
```

Vite forwards those requests to the default backend:

```text
http://localhost:5000
```

Since no backend exists, `ECONNREFUSED` is expected. You can ignore it for now or remove/replace the example API calls when implementing your own frontend.

When your team provides an API later, create `app/.env`:

```env
BACKEND_URL=http://localhost:5000
```

Replace that value with the real backend URL, restart Vite, and the proxy will forward requests there.
